\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{n}
for the
n
-fold application of
\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 M 4.77 3.57 C 4.77 6.21 2.63 8.34 0 8.34 C -2.63 8.34 -4.77 6.21 -4.77 3.57 C -4.77 0.94 -2.63 -1.19 0 -1.19 C 2.63 -1.19 4.77 0.94 4.77 3.57 Z M 0 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
.
This formula requires that for every trace
\pi_{1}
, some trace
\pi_{2}
“predicts
\pi_{1}
”, i.e.,
a
should hold in the
i
th step on
\pi_{2}
iff it holds in the
(i+n)
th step on
\pi_{1}
.
It is easy to see that
\mathcal{T}\models\varphi_{n}
.
However, for
n>1
,
\mathfrak{V}\not\models\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi_{n},1}
, i.e., the verifier looses
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi_{n},1}
.
The reason for this is simple:
In our game, we construct
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
step-wise, so the verifier has to decide on a successor state for the
\pi_{2}
window, knowing only a prefix of
\pi_{1}
.
However, to ensures that
\varphi_{n}
holds, the verifier would need to know which state the refuter will pick
n
steps into the future.

As
\varphi_{n}
contains a single stuttering per trace variable, it seems like there is no need to use a window size larger than
1
.
However, it turns out that the window size has a secondary benefit (beyond accommodating multiple stutterings on the same trace): It provides clairvoyance to the verifier.
In
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi,Z}
, we always maintain a window of size
Z
for the universally quantified traces (cf.
Figure

6
).
The verifier thus does not only see the first positions in the state window, but also the next
Z
steps.
For property
\varphi_{n}
, knowing the next
Z=n
steps is exactly the information that was missing:
It is easy to see that the verifier wins
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi_{n},n}
, i.e., knowing the next
n
steps provides enough information to predict
\pi_{1}
.

Appendix F
Completeness For Admissible Formulas

In this section, we prove
Theorem

6.4
:

See
6.4

We assume
\varphi=\forall\pi_{1}\ldots\forall\pi_{n}\mathpunct{.}\exists\beta_{1}\stutter\pi_{1}\ldots\exists\beta_{n}\stutter\pi_{n}\mathpunct{.}\psi

In
Section

6.3
, we already provide a rough outline and sketch the strategy that wins
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi,1}
.
We recall some of the definitions from
Section

6.3
:
Each vertex
v
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi,1}
controlled by
\mathfrak{V}
has the form

\displaystyle v=\big\langle\hbox to9.02pt{\vbox to9.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.50859pt\lower-4.50859pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.96 0 C 5.96 3.29 3.29 5.96 0 5.96 C -3.29 5.96 -5.96 3.29 -5.96 0 C -5.96 -3.29 -3.29 -5.96 0 -5.96 C 3.29 -5.96 5.96 -3.29 5.96 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.26389pt}{-2.43054pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

\displaystyle\big[\pi_{i}\mapsto[s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}]\big]_{i=1}^{n},\big[\beta_{i}\mapsto 0\big]_{i=1}^{n},\emptyset,q\big\rangle,

i.e., each trace
\pi_{i}
is mapped to a length-
2
state window
[s_{i},s^{\prime}_{i}]
, and the
\beta_{i}
-stuttering points to the
0
th position in this window.
We assume that
v
satisfies all coloring constraints in
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
, i.e., for all
i<j
and
a\in P_{i,j}
,
a\in\ell_{\mathcal{T}}(s_{i})(a)=\ell_{\mathcal{T}}(s_{j})(a)
(if this is not the case,
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
is already violated).
Call this assumption
(A)
.
For
M\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we define states
\mathit{next}^{M}_{v}(1),\ldots,\mathit{next}^{M}_{v}(n)\in S_{\mathcal{T}}
by
\mathit{next}^{M}_{v}(i)=s_{i}
if
i\not\in M
and
\mathit{next}^{M}_{v}(i)=s^{\prime}_{i}
if
i\in M
.
A progress set
M
is
safe
if for every
i<j
, and every
a\in P_{i,j}
,
\ell_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathit{next}^{M}_{v}(i))(a)=\ell_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathit{next}^{M}_{v}(j))(a)
, i.e.,
M
ensures that all coloring constraints are satisfied
locally
in the next step.

See
6.5

Proof.

To show that
M_{1}\cup M_{2}
is safe, let
i<j
be arbitrary.
We claim that for every
a\in P_{i,j}
,
\ell_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(i))(a)=\ell_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(j))(a)
, i.e,
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(i)
and
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(j)
have the same
P_{i,j}
-color.
We perform a case distinction on
(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\cap\{i,j\}
:

•

If
(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\cap\{i,j\}=\emptyset
: That is, none of the two copies are progressed in
M_{1}\cup M_{2}
, so
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(i)=s_{i}
and
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(j)=s_{j}
, and the claim follows directly from assumption
(A)
.

•

If
(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\cap\{i,j\}=\{i\}
(or, analogously,
(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\cap\{i,j\}=\{j\}
):
This implies that
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(i)=s_{i}^{\prime}
and
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(j)=s_{j}
.
Now
(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\cap\{i,j\}=\{i\}
implies that
M_{1}\cap\{i,j\}=\{i\}
or
M_{2}\cap\{i,j\}=\{i\}
.
W.l.o.g., we assume the former, so
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(i)=s_{i}^{\prime}=\mathit{next}^{M_{1}}_{v}(i)
and
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(j)=s_{j}=\mathit{next}^{M_{1}}_{v}(j)
.
By the assumption that
M_{1}
is a safe progress set, we get that
s_{i}^{\prime}
and
s_{j}
have the same
P_{i,j}
-color.

•

If
(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\cap\{i,j\}=\{i,j\}
:
This can occur in two cases (excluding symmetric cases):

–

If
M_{1}\cap\{i,j\}=\{i,j\}
(or, analogously,
M_{2}\cap\{i,j\}=\{i,j\}
): The claim then follows directly from the assumption that
M_{1}
is safe.

–

M_{1}\cap\{i,j\}=\{i\}
and
M_{2}\cap\{i,j\}=\{j\}
(or, analogously,
M_{1}\cap\{i,j\}=\{j\}
and
M_{2}\cap\{i,j\}=\{i\}
):
As
M_{1}
is safe, we have that
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}}_{v}(i)=s_{i}^{\prime}
has the same
P_{i,j}
-color as
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}}_{v}(j)=s_{j}
.
Likewise, as
M_{2}
is safe, we have that
\mathit{next}^{M_{2}}_{v}(i)=s_{i}
has the same
P_{i,j}
-color as
\mathit{next}^{M_{2}}_{v}(j)=s_{j}^{\prime}
.
Moreover, by
(A)

s_{i}
has the same
P_{i,j}
-color as
s_{j}
.
Together this implies that
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(i)=s_{i}^{\prime}
has the same
P_{i,j}
-color as
\mathit{next}^{M_{1}\cup M_{2}}_{v}(j)=s_{j}^{\prime}
as desired.
∎

We can now formally define a winning strategy for the verifier.
For simplicity, we sketch a non-positional strategy, i.e., a strategy that does not only depend on the current vertex but the previous finite play.
As Büchi (and parity) games ar positionally determined
(
Martin 1975
)
, this already implies the existence of a positional strategy (cf.
Section

3
).

In the initial vertex in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi,1}
, the verifier can observe the initial state window of all traces
\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{n}
.
This suffices to evaluate all state formulas in
\psi
(as they only depend on the first state).
Based on this evaluation, the verifier can decide if satisfying
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
is necessary to satisfy
\psi
.

•

If this is not the case, i.e.,
\psi
already holds based on the state formulas alone, the verifier repeatedly progresses all stutterings
\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{n}
. This ensures some fair stuttering and satisfying
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
is not necessary anyway.

•

If not, i.e., the verifier needs to satisfy
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
in order to fulfill
\psi
, it needs to progress such that the color sequences align.
As already outlined in
Section

6.3
, we archive this by using
Lemma

6.5
and always progressing as many traces as possible.
For each vertex
v
, we define
M^{\mathit{max}}_{v}
as the unique maximal safe progress set in vertex
v
(defined as the union of all safe progress sets), which is safe according to
Lemma

6.5
.
For each vertex
v
, the verifier now progresses all traces in
M^{\mathit{max}}_{v}
, i.e., we define

\displaystyle\sigma^{\mathit{max}}(v):=\big\langle\hbox to9.21pt{\vbox to9.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60332pt\lower-4.60332pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.09 0 C 6.09 3.37 3.37 6.09 0 6.09 C -3.37 6.09 -6.09 3.37 -6.09 0 C -6.09 -3.37 -3.37 -6.09 0 -6.09 C 3.37 -6.09 6.09 -3.37 6.09 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.40627pt}{-2.43054pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},\big[\pi_{i}\mapsto[s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}]\big]_{i=1}^{n},\big[\beta_{i}\mapsto\mathit{ite}(i\in M^{\mathit{max}}_{v},1,0)\big]_{i=1}^{n},\{\beta_{i}\mid i\in M_{v}^{\mathit{max}}\},q\big\rangle.

That is, the verifier always (locally) progresses as many stutterings as possible while ensuring that the coloring constraints in
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
holds in the next step.

Let
\sigma^{\mathit{max}}
be the resulting strategy for the verifier.
It is not hard to see that
\sigma^{\mathit{max}}
wins
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi,1}
:
Strategy
\sigma^{\mathit{max}}
always progresses as many stutterings as possible in order to satisfy the coloring constraints in
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
.
On any paths
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
for
\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{n}
that satisfy
\exists\beta_{1}\stutter\pi_{1},\ldots,\beta_{n}\stutter\pi_{n}\mathpunct{.}\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
, strategy
\sigma^{\mathit{max}}
automatically aligns them such that
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
holds.
That is each trace is progressed as long as possible and if it cannot be progressed without violating
\psi_{\mathit{phase}}
, it automatically “waits” until it can change its color
together
with the other traces.
Consequently, we get:

Lemma F.1.

By following strategy
\sigma^{\mathit{max}}
, the verifier wins
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{T},\varphi,1}
.
